BRANCH 1: Geometry‑type & phrase semantics  
The vernacular “intersect at Jacksboro” indicates that the highways (LineString) pass through the area of the town (Polygon). In OGC spatial predicates, a LineString that enters and exits a Polygon is said to **cross** the Polygon. The other predicates are incompatible:  
- **contains** would require the highway to enclose the town, which is impossible for a line.  
- **within** would mean the entire highway lies inside the town, which is not the case for major highways.  
- **touches** would mean the highway only meets the town boundary, but “intersect at” implies passage through the interior.  
- **overlaps** and **equals** are irrelevant for line‑polygon pairs.  
- **disjoint** contradicts the stated intersection.  

Suggested predicate: **crosses**

The phrase “intersect at Jacksboro” therefore reflects the common routing of these highways through the municipal boundary. Since the highways are linear features and the town is an area, the only spatial relation that captures a linear feature passing through an area is **crosses**.  

Suggested predicate: **crosses**

The expression “intersect at Jacksboro” is a direct statement that the highways and the town share a common point of intersection. In spatial terms, this means the LineString of the highways intersects the Polygon of the town. The OGC definition of **crosses** applies when the intersection is not empty and the line passes through the interior of the polygon.
